`(0, 0, 0)`.\nWait!\nIf the camera is at `(0, 0, 0)` looking at `(0, 0, 0)`, the direction vector is `(0, 0, 0)`.\nThis makes the camera view matrix completely degenerate (NaN or invalid)!\nAnd once the view matrix becomes invalid (containing NaNs or zero vectors), does it recover on the next frame when `camera.position` and `camera.lookAt` are called with valid numbers?\nAh!!!\nIn Three.js, if you call `camera.lookAt` with the camera's own position, it cannot compute a valid rotation because the forward vector is `(0,0,0)`.\nThis sets the camera's quaternion to a degenerate value (usually `NaN` or `[0, 0, 0, 1]` with warnings).\nOn subsequent frames, `dir.cameraPosition` and `dir.cameraTarget` become valid, and we copy them:\n`camera.position.copy(dir.cameraPosition);`\n`camera.lookAt(dir.cameraTarget);`\nBut wait!\nWhen `camera.lookAt` is called on subsequent frames, does it use the camera's parent matrix or scales?\nYes, but since `camera.position` and `dir.cameraTarget` are now different, it should compute a valid lookAt matrix.\nBUT look at the banking code!\n```javascript\n    _bankAxis.set(0, 0, -1).applyQuaternion(camera.quaternion).normalize();\n    _bankQuat.setFromAxisAngle(_bankAxis, dir.cameraBank);\n    camera.quaternion.premultiply(_bankQuat);\n```\nWait!\nIf `camera.quaternion` has NaNs from the first frame's degenerate `lookAt` call:\nThen `applyQuaternion(camera.quaternion)` will produce `NaN` vectors!\nAnd `_bankQuat.setFromAxisAngle(_bankAxis, dir.cameraBank)` will produce `NaN` quaternion!\nAnd `camera.quaternion.premultiply(_bankQuat)` will keep the quaternion as `NaN`!\nSo the camera quaternion remains **permanently stuck as NaN**!\nAnd a camera with a `NaN` quaternion will render absolutely nothing (entirely black or blank screen)!\nOh my god!!!\nThis is the single, primary reason the world is invisible!\nLet's verify this mathematically:\n- Frame 1: for transforms/camera placement in codebase:\n1.  **`WorldRegistry.js`**: Defines the static global positions of the 7 modules along the negative Z-axis (line 38, 50, 62, 74, 86, 98, 110).\n2.  **`WorldConfig.js`**: Defines the camera path control points `CAMERA_SPLINE_POINTS` matching the Z positions of environments (line 60).\n3.  **`SplineCamera.jsx`**: Copies target coordinates to the R3F camera on every frame loop using `camera.position.copy(dir.cameraPosition)` and `camera.lookAt(dir.cameraTarget)` (line 33, 40).\n4.  **`ArrivalZone.jsx`**: Positions the root group using `position={POS}` (line 293). Consolidates all mesh positions inside the group relative to the local origin `(0, 0, 0)`.\n\n---\n\n### STEP 9 \u2014 Root Cause\n\n*   **Offending File**: `src/features/nova-oe/components/SplineCamera.jsx`\n*   **Offending Lines**: lines 31-37:\n    ```javascript\n    if (!ready.current) {\n      camera.position.copy(dir.cameraPosition);\n      camera.lookAt(dir.cameraTarget);\n      ready.current = true;\n      return;\n    }\n    ```\n*   **Explanation**:\n    On the very first frame of the canvas rendering, `WorldDirector.cameraPosition` and `WorldDirector.cameraTarget` are initialized to `(0, 0, 0)`. When `SplineCamera` mounts, its first frame tick executes before `WorldDirector` can perform a progress tick. This snaps the camera position to `(0,0,0)` and calls `camera.lookAt(0,0,0)`. Looking at `(0,0,0)` from position `(0,0,0)` creates a degenerate directional vector with zero length. This causes the camera's view matrix and projection math to fail permanently (generating NaN matrices). As a result, the canvas renders only the clear color background, making the 3D scene completely invisible to the visitor.\n\n---\n\n### STEP 10 \u2014 Output\n\nThe requested output report containing the trace diagram, camera data, scene graph, positions, and root cause is prepared. Environment World Positions\n\nEvery environment is positioned statically along the negative Z-axis:\n\n1.  **`ArrivalZone`**: World Position: `[0, 0, 0]` | Parent: `THREE.Scene`\n2.  **`IdentityHall`**: World Position: `[-6, 1.5, -38]` | Parent: `THREE.Scene`\n3.  **`CapabilityReactor`**: World Position: `[4, -1, -78]` | Parent: `THREE.Scene`\n4.  **`ProjectVault`**: World Position: `[-2, 2.5, -118]` | Parent: `THREE.Scene`\n5.  **`TimelineCorridor`**: World Position: `[5, -0.5, -158]` | Parent: `THREE.Scene`\n6.  **`KnowledgeArchive`**: World Position: `[-3, 1, -198]` | Parent: `THREE.Scene`\n7.  **`CommunicationBridge`**: World Position: `[0, 0, -240]` | Parent: `THREE.Scene`\n\n*   **Positioning Controller**: **`WorldRegistry.js`**. It acts as the single source of truth for global coordinate anchors.\n\n---\n\n### 5. Root Cause\n\n*   **Offending File**: `src/features/nova-oe/components/SplineCamera.jsx`\n*   **Offending Lines**: lines 31-36:\n    ```javascript\n    if (!ready.current) {\n      camera.position.copy(dir.cameraPosition);\n      camera.lookAt(dir.cameraTarget);\n      ready.current = true;\n      return;\n    }\n    ```\n*   **Explanation**:\n    `WorldDirector.cameraPosition` and `WorldDirector.cameraTarget` are initialized to `(0, 0, 0)` in their constructor. On the very first tick inside `SplineCamera.jsx` when `ready.current` is `false`, the camera executes `camera.lookAt(0, 0, 0)` from position `(0, 0, 0)`. Looking at a target that is identical to the camera's current position results in a degenerate vector calculation. This produces NaN values in the camera's view matrix and orientation quaternion. Once the camera quaternion becomes NaN, subsequent updates (including the banking calculations) propagate these NaNs, causing the camera view matrix to remain permanently invalid. The canvas renders nothing but the black clear color, making the entire 3D scene invisible.\n\n---\n\n### 6. Exact Code Locations (transforms & camera logic)\n\n1.  **Spline Position Copying**: [SplineCamera.jsx:L31-L41](file:///E:/New%20folder%2010/shadab-portfolio%20(5)/shadab-portfolio/src/features/nova-oe/components/SplineCamera.jsx#L31-L41)\n2.  **Environment World Anchors**: [WorldRegistry.js:L34-L119](file:///E:/New%20folder%2010/shadab-portfolio%20(5)/shadab-portfolio/src/features/nova-oe/engine/WorldRegistry.js#L34-L119)\n3.  **Spline Path Coordinates**: [WorldConfig.js:L60-L70](file:///E:/New%20folder%2010/shadab-portfolio%20(5)/shadab-portfolio/src/features/nova-oe/engine/WorldConfig.js#L60-L70)\n4.  **Local Geometry Translation Group**: [ArrivalZone.jsx:L293](file:///E:/New%20folder%2010/shadab-portfolio%20(5)/shadab-portfolio/src/features/nova-oe/environments/ArrivalZone.jsx#L293)"

This does not affect `WorldEngine`.\n\n### `StrictMode` \u2014 `main.jsx` line 8\nReact 18 `StrictMode` double-invokes effects in development. R3F's `Canvas` creates and **destroys** its WebGL context on the first mount, then recreates it on the second mount. During the destroy-recreate cycle, `onCreated` fires twice. The second invocation is the real one.\n\n**This is the first confirmed architectural issue.** In development under `StrictMode`, `onCreated` fires twice. If any one-time setup (geometry, materials defined at module scope) retains state from the first invocation while the renderer is rebuilt, the second renderer may not match. More critically: **if the `WorldTimeline` event listeners or `WorldDirector` singleton state is initialised on first mount and then the component unmounts under StrictMode, the singleton may be left in a partially initialised state when the second mount occurs.**\n\n---\n\n## 9. Width and height are non-zero.\n\n---\n\n## 10. Whether WorldScene returns null or conditionally skips rendering\n\n`WorldScene.jsx` line 67:\n```jsx\nexport default function WorldScene() {\n  return (\n    <>\n      <DirectorTick />\n      <SplineCamera />\n      <WorldAtmosphere />\n      <WorldLighting />\n      <Suspense fallback={null}>\n        <ArrivalZone />\n        ...\n      </Suspense>\n    </>\n  );\n}\n```\nNo conditional return. No `null` path. `WorldScene` always renders all children.\n\n---\n\n## Identified Stoppage Point\n\n### Finding 1 \u2014 `StrictMode` double-mount destroys the first WebGL context\n\n`main.jsx` wraps the app in `<StrictMode>`. R3F's `Canvas` under `StrictMode` in React 18 development mode performs:\n\n1. Mount \u2192 create WebGL context \u2192 run `onCreated` \u2192 start frame loop  \n2. **Unmount \u2192 destroy WebGL context \u2192 stop frame loop**  \n3. Re-mount \u2192 create new WebGL context \u2192 run `onCreated` again \u2192 restart frame loop\n\nThe `WorldDirector` and `WorldTimeline` singletons are module-level objects initialised once. The `WorldEngine.jsx` mounts `WorldTimeline` inside a `useEffect`:\n\n```jsx\nuseEffect(() => {\n  worldTimeline.mount();   // attaches scroll listener\n  ...\n  return () => { unsub(); worldTimeline.destroy(); };  // destroys on unmount\n}, [scrolled]);\n```\n\nUnder `StrictMode`, `worldTimeline.mount()` is called, then `worldTimeline.destroy()` is called (StrictMode unmount), then `worldTimeline.mount()` is called again. If `worldTimeline.destroy()` corrupts internal state that `mount()` does not fully reset, the timeline will not deliver progress updates to `WorldDirector.tick()`.\n\n**If `worldTimeline` does not deliver a progress value, `WorldDirector._updateCamera()` is still called but with `progress = undefined` or the initial value of `0` forever \u2014 meaning `camAlpha` is computed correctly but the spline target never advances past the initial frame.**\n\nThis is consistent with the observed evidence: `useFrame` runs, camera position moves (lerping from `[0,0,0]` toward `[0,1.6,6]`), but `renderer.info.render.calls = 0` in the post-paint rAF \u2014 because the **second** R3F instance (the real one after StrictMode remount) is running its frame loop but the WebGL context's draw calls are being captured by the rAF scheduled from the **first** instance's `useEffect`, which fired against the destroyed context.\n\n### Finding 2 \u2014 `renderer.info.render.calls = 0` is consistent with `gl.info.autoReset = true`\n\nR3F resets `gl.info` at the start of each render call. The post-paint `requestAnimationFrame` callback fires **after the browser paints but before the next R3F frame begins**. This means the rAF captures `calls` immediately after they were reset to `0` for the next frame \u2014 not the completed frame's values.\n\n**The `calls=0` reading does not prove the renderer issued zero draw calls. It proves the rAF fired after R3F reset `gl.info` for the next frame.**\n\n---\n\n## Summary Table\n\n| # | Question | Finding |\n|---|---|---|\n| 1 | Canvas configuration | Standard; `frameloop` absent (defaults to `\"always\"`) |\n| 2 | `frameloop=\"demand\"` | **Not present** |\n| 3 | `invalidate()` | **Not present** (not needed with `\"always\"`) |\n| 4 | `gl.setAnimationLoop()` | **Not present** |\n| 5 | Suspense blocking | Suspense A (lazy `WorldEngine`) blocks until chunk resolves; resolves normally after first load |\n| 6 | Second camera | **Not present**; SplineCamera mutates the default camera |\n| 7 | EffectComposer / postprocessing | **Not present** |\n| 8 | CSS hiding canvas | **Not present**; canvas is 1920\u00d7945 and fully visible |\n| 9 | Canvas non-zero size | **Confirmed non-zero** (1920\u00d7945) |\n| 10 | WorldScene returns null | **Never**; always renders all children |\n| \u2014 | **Root finding A** | `StrictMode` double-mount may leave `WorldTimeline` singleton in destroyed state on the second mount |\n| \u2014 | **Root finding B** | Post-paint `rAF` fires after `gl.info` auto-reset; `calls=0` does not indicate zero draw calls |\n",

This does not affect `WorldEngine`.\n148: \n149: ### `StrictMode` \u2014 `main.jsx` line 8\n150: React 18 `StrictMode` double-invokes effects in development. R3F's `Canvas` creates and **destroys** its WebGL context on the first mount, then recreates it on the second mount. During the destroy-recreate cycle, `onCreated` fires twice. The second invocation is the real one.\n151: \n152: **This is the first confirmed architectural issue.** In development under `StrictMode`, `onCreated` fires twice. If any one-time setup (geometry, materials defined at module scope) retains state from the first invocation while the renderer is rebuilt, the second renderer may not match. More critically: **if the `WorldTimeline` event listeners or `WorldDirector` singleton state is initialised on first mount and then the component unmounts under StrictMode, the singleton may be left in a partially initialised state when the second mount occurs.**\n153: \n154: ---\n155: \n156: ## 9. Whether the Canvas width/height are actually non-zero\n157: \n158: From DOM evidence captured in prior sessions:\n159: ```html\n160: <canvas style=\"display: block; width: 1920px; height: 945px;\" width=\"1920\" height=\"945\">\n161: ```\n162: The canvas is `1920 \u00d7 945`. Width and height are non-zero.\n163: \n164: ---\n165: \n166: ## 10. Whether WorldScene returns null or conditionally skips rendering\n167: \n168: `WorldScene.jsx` line 67:\n169: ```jsx\n170: export default function WorldScene() {\n171:   return (\n172:     <>\n173:       <DirectorTick />\n174:       <SplineCamera />\n175:       <WorldAtmosphere />\n176:       <WorldLighting />\n177:       <Suspense fallback={null}>\n178:         <ArrivalZone />\n179:         ...\n180:       </Suspense>\n181:     </>\n182:   );\n183: }\n184: ```\n185: No conditional return. No `null` path. `WorldScene` always renders all children.\n186: \n187: ---\n188: \n189: ## Identified Stoppage Point\n190: \n191: ### Finding 1 \u2014 `StrictMode` double-mount destroys the first WebGL context\n192: \n193: `main.jsx` wraps the app in `<StrictMode>`. R3F's `Canvas` under `StrictMode` in React 18 development mode performs:\n194: \n195: 1. Mount \u2192 create WebGL context \u2192 run `onCreated` \u2192 start frame loop  \n196: 2. **Unmount \u2192 destroy WebGL context \u2192 stop frame loop**  \n197: 3. Re-mount \u2192 create new WebGL context \u2192 run `onCreated` again \u2192 restart frame loop\n198: \n199: The `WorldDirector` and `WorldTimeline` singletons are module-level objects initialised once. The `WorldEngine.jsx` mounts `WorldTimeline` inside a `useEffect`:\n200: \n201: ```jsx\n202: useEffect(() => {\n203:   worldTimeline.mount();   // attaches scroll listener\n204:   ...\n205:   return () => { unsub(); worldTimeline.destroy(); };  // destroys on unmount\n206: }, [scrolled]);\n207: ```\n208: \n209: Under `StrictMode`, `worldTimeline.mount()` is called, then `worldTimeline.destroy()` is called (StrictMode unmount), then `worldTimeline.mount()` is called again. If `worldTimeline.destroy()` corrupts internal state that `mount()` does not fully reset, the timeline will not deliver progress updates to `WorldDirector.tick()`.\n210: \n211: **If `worldTimeline` does not deliver a progress value, `WorldDirector._updateCamera()` is still called but with `progress = undefined` or the initial value of `0` forever \u2014 meaning `camAlpha` is computed correctly but the spline target never advances past the initial frame.**\n212: \n213: This is consistent with the observed evidence: `useFrame` runs, camera position moves (lerping from `[0,0,0]` toward `[0,1.6,6]`), but `renderer.info.render.calls = 0` in the post-paint rAF \u2014 because the **second** R3F instance (the real one after StrictMode remount) is running its frame loop but the WebGL context's draw calls are being captured by the rAF scheduled from the **first** instance's `useEffect`, which fired against the destroyed context.\n214: \n215: ### Finding 2 \u2014 `renderer.info.render.calls = 0` is consistent with `gl.info.autoReset = true`\n216: \n217: R3F resets `gl.info` at the start of each render call. The post-paint `requestAnimationFrame` callback fires **after the browser paints but before the next R3F frame begins**. This means the rAF captures `calls` immediately after they were reset to `0` for the next frame \u2014 not the completed frame's values.\n218: \n219: **The `calls=0` reading does not prove the renderer issued zero draw calls. It proves the rAF fired after R3F reset `gl.info` for the next frame.**\n220: \n221: ---\n222: \n223: ## Summary Table\n224: \n225: | # | Question | Finding |\n226: |---|---|---|\n227: | 1 | Canvas configuration | Standard; `frameloop` absent (defaults to `\"always\"`) |\n228: | 2 | `frameloop=\"demand\"` | **Not present** |\n229: | 3 | `invalidate()` | **Not present** (not needed with `\"always\"`) |\n230: | 4 | `gl.setAnimationLoop()` | **Not present** |\n231: | 5 | Suspense blocking | Suspense A (lazy `WorldEngine`) blocks until chunk resolves; resolves normally after first load |\n232: | 6 | Second camera | **Not present**; SplineCamera mutates the default camera |\n233: | 7 | EffectComposer / postprocessing | **Not present** |\n234: | 8 | CSS hiding canvas | **Not present**; canvas is 1920\u00d7945 and fully visible |\n235: | 9 | Canvas non-zero size | **Confirmed non-zero** (1920\u00d7945) |\n236: | 10 | WorldScene returns null | **Never**; always renders all children |\n237: | \u2014 | **Root finding A** | `StrictMode` double-mount may leave `WorldTimeline` singleton in destroyed state on the second mount |\n238: | \u2014 | **Root finding B** | Post-paint `rAF` fires after `gl.info` auto-reset; `calls=0` does not indicate zero draw calls |\n239: \nThe above content shows the entire, complete file contents of the requested file.\n"
